None of these statements quantifies autonomy (no success rate, no interventions per hour, no autonomous time share) [analyst observation on the quoted pages].

#### 10.2 What the official tutorials require of a human (VERIFIED)

| Step the human performs | Manual text | Taxonomy mapping (proposed) | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Set initial pose with the RViz 2D Pose Estimate tool | "This is required for the Nav2 stack to know where to start localizing from. Click on the tool, and then click and drag" | `hint` (hint_as_event) or `shared`/`human_initiated` under hint_as_shared, scope `base`; task not autonomous (definitions §6.2) | VERIFIED: https://turtlebot.github.io/turtlebot4-user-manual/tutorials/navigation.html#setting-the-initial-pose and https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/navigation.md#L220, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Click a Nav2 Goal in RViz | "The Nav2 Goal tool allows you to set a goal pose for the robot. The Nav2 stack will then plan a path" | goal issued before `task_start` = task definition, not a hint (§6.2); goals issued mid-task = `hint` | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/navigation.md#L247, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Drive the robot by keyboard or joystick to build a SLAM map | "Use any method to drive the robot around the area you wish to map" | `human_local` or `human_remote` per device registry (§6.10); mapping session is human time | VERIFIED: https://turtlebot.github.io/turtlebot4-user-manual/tutorials/generate_map.html and https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/generate_map.md#L130, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Keyboard teleop sends to `/cmd_vel` | "Both the keyboard and joystick teleop methods work by sending velocity commands the the robot through the `/cmd_vel` topic" | observer can see human input on `/cmd_vel` only when no autonomy publisher is active; the stack has no mux (lead grep) | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/driving.md#L99, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Joystick dead-man: hold L1/R1 while moving the stick | "press and hold either **L1** or **R1**, and move the left joystick" | human command only while the enable button is held; releasing it is a hold caused by the human (§6.8 → `shared`, `human_initiated`) if a task is open | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/driving.md#L95, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Jazzy keyboard teleop must be launched with `stamped:=true` | "the `stamped` parameter must be set to `true`" | observer should expect `TwistStamped` on `cmd_vel` from humans on Jazzy | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/driving.md#L57 and #L61, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Dock/undock | Navigator examples call `navigator.undock()` / `navigator.dock()` before and after navigation; Create 3 buttons: "Pressing button 1 will direct the robot to dock … button 2 will direct the robot to undock" | scripted command issued by a program = `ai` (Create 3 docking is sensor-driven); button press = `human_local` initiating a `scripted`/`ai` behaviour; see Uncovered cases | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/turtlebot4_navigator.md#L137 and https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/first_node_cpp.md#L161, acc. 2026-10-31 |
| Save map | `ros2 service call /slam_toolbox/save_map` | between-task human action, `planned` reset if pre-registered | VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/generate_map.md#L145, acc. 2026-10-31 |

The Create 3 base also contributes autonomous reflexes that "will temporarily override any user-provided command for their short duration", enabled by default (`reflexes_enabled` true; REFLEX_BUMP, CLIFF, GYRO_CAL, PANIC, PROXIMITY_SLOWDOWN, STUCK, VIRTUAL_WALL, WHEEL_DROP enabled; REFLEX_DOCK_AVOID disabled) [VERIFIED: https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/api/reflexes/ and https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/reflexes.md#L5, #L14, #L22, #L38, acc. 2026-10-31]. With `safety_override = none` (default) the speed is limited to 0.306 m/s; `full` allows 0.460 m/s [VERIFIED: https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/api/safety/ and https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/safety.md#L67 and #L69, acc. 2026-10-31]. The `e_stop` service turns the wheels off and "The robot will not respond to velocity commands when `e_stop_on` is true" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/safety.md#L79-L80, acc. 2026-10-31].

#### 10.3 How autonomy is measured today for this robot class

1. **Nav2 recovery count.** `NavigateToPose` feedback carries `int16 number_of_recoveries` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_msgs/action/NavigateToPose.action#L18, acc. 2026-10-31], set from the behaviour-tree navigator's `recovery_count` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_bt_navigator/src/navigators/navigate_to_pose.cpp#L189, acc. 2026-10-31] and shown in the RViz Nav2 panel [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_rviz_plugins/src/nav2_panel.cpp#L1471, acc. 2026-10-31]. Recoveries are AI behaviours (state `ai`), not interventions; they are a proxy for difficulty, not for human help. (The Nav2 track owns this signal; it is listed here only as the measurement practice.)
2. **Marathon 2 (Macenski et al. 2020).** Metrics in Table III: total time 22.8 h, distance 37.4 miles, recoveries 168 (4.3 per mile as printed in Table III (the pooled figure 168/37.4 = 4.49 per mile; the paper's "total" is the unweighted mean of the two robots' rates 3.3 and 5.3 — re-checked against the PDF 2026-11-03)), average speed 0.37 m/s, max speed 0.45 m/s, collisions 0, emergency stops 0; the text states the robots "operated without direct assistance throughout the experiment" [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.00368 §V.B Table III, acc. 2026-11-03]. Per-second log fields: `timestamp, distance, recovery_executed, vel_x, vel_theta` [VERIFIED: same paper §V.A, acc. 2026-11-03]. No definition of "assistance" or of an intervention is given in the paper; this is exactly the gap the Autonomy Meter taxonomy fills.
3. **BARN (Perille et al. 2020).** 300 generated environments; difficulty = traversal time averaged over trials and normalised by path length, with a 30 s penalty when the goal is not reached; simulated Clearpath Jackal in Gazebo with DWA and E-Band; 3 000 simulated and 50 physical trials [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.13315 §IV.A–B, acc. 2026-11-03]. Not TB4.
4. **BARN Challenge reports (ICRA 2022–2024).** The 3rd-challenge report defines the per-environment score as the success indicator times a clipped ratio of optimal to actual traversal time, averaged over 50 unseen environments with 10 trials each; the standardised robot is a Clearpath Jackal with a 2 m/s maximum speed, and a run counts as success only "without any collisions with obstacles or any human interventions" [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01862 §I–II, acc. 2026-11-03]. Earlier reports: https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.10473 (2022), https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.03205 (2023) [CLAIM: abstracts only; journal versions not opened]. "Human intervention" is used as a binary failure condition per trial, which matches the project's task-level `task_autonomy_rate` but not its time share.
5. **SocNavBench (Biswas et al. 2021).** Overall success rate "the fraction of episodes successfully completed (robot reached goal position and did not collide with any pedestrian)" plus path-length ratio, path irregularity and time metrics; simulation only [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.00047 §5 metrics table, acc. 2026-11-03]. Not TB4.
6. **NIST ALFUS HI metric** — covered elsewhere in the project; one line: it rates human independence qualitatively and is not computed from logs.

Summary: the published practice for this robot class measures success rate, recoveries and collisions; none of the sources above logs who held control over time, and none was recorded on a TurtleBot 4 [analyst synthesis of items 1–5].

### Peer-reviewed context  _[Nav2 track]_

Macenski, Martín, White and Ginés Clavero, "The Marathon 2: A Navigation System", IROS 2020 (arXiv 2003.00368) describes the behavior-tree navigation with a recovery subtree (clear costmap, spin, wait, back up) that this track documents. Its Section V.B, Table III, reports for the two robots (RB-1, TIAGo) a total of 22.8 h, 37.4 miles, 168 recoveries (4.3 per mile as printed in Table III (the pooled figure 168/37.4 = 4.49 per mile; the paper's "total" is the unweighted mean of the two robots' rates 3.3 and 5.3 — re-checked against the PDF 2026-11-03); 52 for RB-1 at 3.3/mile, 116 for TIAGo at 5.3/mile), 0 collisions and 0 emergency stops; the text states "The robots operated without direct assistance throughout the experiment and never failed to complete a navigation task", and that "Passive assistance was rendered to the robot on rare instances when students occupied the exact pose of a waypoint", with a time impact "under 1 minute over the 22.8 hour experiment"; the robot logged `recovery_executed` once a second [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.00368, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.00368v2, acc. 2026-10-31]. In this taxonomy those 168 recoveries are `ai` time with `x_recovery` flags (not interventions), and the passive assistance (people asked to step aside) is neither a transition nor a hint — a case the taxonomy does not cover (Uncovered 8).

Macenski, Moore, Lu, Merzlyakov and Ferguson, "From the desks of ROS maintainers: A survey of modern & capable mobile robotics algorithms in the robot operating system 2", Robotics and Autonomous Systems 2023 (arXiv 2307.15236) does **not** contain intervention, disengagement or recovery-rate metrics; its only related content is listing "Assisted Teleop" as a Nav2 behavior ("Tele-op with collision avoidance") [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.15236, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.15236, acc. 2026-10-31].

## 11. Threat model: making human-driven work look autonomous on this platform

_Lead note._ The stack track's table is the core. The base and Nav2 tracks add four threats that the lead consolidates here: (a) **BLE control plane** — the Create 3 adapter-board USB/BLE toggle lets the iRobot Education Bluetooth protocol drive the base with reflexes disabled and no ROS 2 trace; exposed only by wheel motion (`/odom`, `/wheel_vels`) with no `/cmd_vel` publisher and no action goal (base track Section 4 and 14C). (b) **Webserver boot parameters** — `safety_override`, `reflexes_enabled`, `publish_hazard_msgs=false`, `raw_kinematics_min_pub_period_ms=-1` can be preset over unauthenticated HTTP so that safety stops never appear in the log; exposed by `/parameter_events` after restart and by recording the parameter set at `session_start` (`x_safety_config`, UC-19). (c) **Fake Nav2 status** — any node can publish `/navigate_to_pose/_action/status`, `/behavior_tree_log` or `/collision_monitor_state`; exposed by requiring consistent `/cmd_vel_nav` → `/cmd_vel_smoothed` → `/cmd_vel` payload equality and odometry (Nav2 track Section 6). (d) **Scripted replay presented as autonomy** — the `turtlebot4_tests` tool and any `drive_distance`/`rotate_angle` script are fixed macros (`scripted`); exposed by action goals from a test/script node and motion that does not react to a perturbation (definitions 6.9).

### 11. Threat model specific to TurtleBot 4  _[TB4 stack track]_

| Threat | Mechanism (evidence) | Data that exposes it |
|---|---|---|
| Teleop from a hidden machine | any participant on `ROS_DOMAIN_ID` 0 (default) can publish `/cmd_vel`; no mux [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/setup.bash#L4, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/driving.md#L99, acc. 2026-10-31] | publisher GID/node list of `/cmd_vel` at session start and per message; `/odom` motion with no logged Nav2 output (`/cmd_vel_nav` silent) |
| Renamed node impersonating Nav2 | node name is a CLI flag (`--ros-args -r __node:=collision_monitor`); only GID and process host differ | GID set recorded at `session_start`; two publishers with the same node name; `/cmd_vel` payloads not equal to `/cmd_vel_smoothed` |
| Bluetooth controller | Bluetooth terminates at the Pi; `/joy` and `cmd_vel` appear on DDS from the Pi [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_robot/blob/28e115f86930bb3273f1a1aa4738dbd8747513f0/turtlebot4_bringup/launch/joy_teleop.launch.py#L53, acc. 2026-10-31] | `/joy` (if recorded) and `/cmd_vel` from `teleop_twist_joy_node`; `frame_id` `teleop_twist_joy` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/teleop_twist_joy/blob/556a07100ee745f68619de84a95d3c84a4892ab9/src/teleop_twist_joy.cpp#L94, acc. 2026-10-31]; `/function_calls` for B/L2/R2/Home; invisible if the Pi is on a discovery server the observer does not join |
| Create 3 webserver / app config | webserver on the Create 3 at `192.168.186.2:80` forwarded to Pi port 8080 by `socat` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/systemd/system/webserver.service#L9, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/troubleshooting/factory_reset.md#L22, acc. 2026-10-31]; it changes firmware parameters and ROS settings (Create 3 track) | no ROS topic; only effects (e.g. namespace/RMW change -> observer loses topics) |
| Create 3 faceplate buttons | dock/undock/wall-follow/e-stop/power without any network [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_robot/blob/28e115f86930bb3273f1a1aa4738dbd8747513f0/turtlebot4_bringup/config/turtlebot4.yaml#L42-L44, acc. 2026-10-31] | `/interface_buttons`, `/function_calls`, `/dock_status`, `/wheel_status`, action status |
| Display menu | Select triggers Dock/Undock/EStop/Wall Follow/Power [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_robot/blob/28e115f86930bb3273f1a1aa4738dbd8747513f0/turtlebot4_bringup/config/turtlebot4.yaml#L53, acc. 2026-10-31] | `/hmi/buttons`, `/hmi/display.selected_entry`, `/function_calls` |
| Post-hoc bag editing | MCAP/sqlite3 files are plain files owned by the recording user; the rosbag2 README documents compression and storage options but no integrity or signing feature [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/README.md, acc. 2026-10-31] | Autonomy Meter hash chain (definitions.md Section 8) written live by the observer, not by rosbag2 |
| Fake Nav2 action feedback | any node can host or spoof `/navigate_to_pose` feedback (Nav2 track) | cross-check with `/cmd_vel_nav` presence and `/odom` |
| Clock manipulation | Pi system clock is disciplined by chrony from Ubuntu NTP pools and serves the Create 3 over usb0 (`allow 192.168.186.0/24`, `local stratum 10`) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L16-L17, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L23, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L26, acc. 2026-10-31]; `makestep 1 3` allows steps at start [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L51, acc. 2026-10-31]; Create 3 syncs from `192.168.186.3` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L8, acc. 2026-10-31]. Raspberry Pi documentation describes the RTC as a Raspberry Pi 5 feature [VERIFIED: https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/daf1ca791713d5f1346556122a2b6942bcc7b1d7/documentation/asciidoc/computers/raspberry-pi/rtc.adoc#L3, acc. 2026-10-31]; [UNKNOWN: explicit statement that the Pi 4 has no RTC was not found in an opened primary text (inferred from the Pi 5 wording and chrony local stratum fallback)] | recorder `ts` from the observer's own NTP-synced clock; `received_timestamp` vs `source_timestamp` skew per publisher; `/clock` absent on hardware |
| `ROS_DOMAIN_ID` isolation | changing the Pi's domain (turtlebot4_setup menu `ROS_DOMAIN_ID`) hides all topics from an observer on another domain [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_setup/ros_setup.py#L116, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_discovery/configure_discovery.sh#L32, acc. 2026-10-31] | session_start must record the domain; absence of `/ip` heartbeat (0.2 Hz) = lost robot |
| Discovery-server hiding | with `ROS_DISCOVERY_SERVER` set, participants that do not register with the server (or without `ROS_SUPER_CLIENT`) see nothing [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/setup/discovery_server.md#L226, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_discovery/configure_discovery.sh#L178, acc. 2026-10-31] | observer must be configured as super client; log `ROS_DISCOVERY_SERVER` value at session_start |
| Direct publish into `_do_not_use` | Create 3 topics exist in `<ns>/_do_not_use`; a node on the Pi (or on the Create 3's Wi-Fi if enabled) can publish there, bypassing the republished `/cmd_vel` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/software/create3.md#L191, acc. 2026-10-31] | record `/_do_not_use/cmd_vel_stamped` from the Pi as well; `/odom` motion without `/cmd_vel` |
| SSH keyboard teleop on the Pi | default credentials (Section 12) | `/cmd_vel` publisher `teleop_twist_keyboard` with the Pi's GID prefix; cannot be told from a PC publisher by node name |

## 12. Security and privacy

### 12. Security, privacy, remote access (TB4 layer)  _[TB4 stack track]_

- **SSH defaults.** Manual: "ssh ubuntu@10.42.0.1 ... Log in using the password `turtlebot4`" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/setup/basic.md#L84, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/setup/basic.md#L87, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://turtlebot.github.io/turtlebot4-user-manual/setup/basic.html, acc. 2026-10-31]. `discovery.conf` runs the server as `User=ubuntu` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/discovery.conf#L6, acc. 2026-10-31].
- **Wi-Fi AP defaults.** First boot: AP mode SSID `Turtlebot4`, password `Turtlebot4`, 5 GHz; Pi at 10.42.0.1 [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/setup/basic.md#L73, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/setup/basic.md#L76, acc. 2026-10-31]; netplan `50-wifis.yaml`: `Turtlebot4` / `mode: ap` / `password: Turtlebot4` / band 5GHz [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/netplan/50-wifis.yaml#L7, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/netplan/50-wifis.yaml#L10, acc. 2026-10-31]; `conf.py` defaults `SSID: 'Turtlebot4'`, `PASSWORD: 'Turtlebot4'` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_setup/conf.py#L97, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_setup/conf.py#L98, acc. 2026-10-31]. Wired fallback: eth0 192.168.185.3/24, usb0 192.168.186.3/24 [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/netplan/40-ethernets.yaml#L7, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/netplan/40-ethernets.yaml#L12, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/troubleshooting/faq.md#L56, acc. 2026-10-31].
- **Discovery server port.** 11811 default (`fastdds discovery -i 0 -p 11811`) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/discovery.sh#L3, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_setup/conf.py#L120, acc. 2026-10-31].
- **DDS security.** No `ROS_SECURITY_*` variables, no SROS2 keystore and no `security` element in any TB4 config (grep of turtlebot4_setup for `SECURITY|sros|keystore`: 0 hits) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/setup.bash#L1, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/fastdds_rpi.xml#L4, acc. 2026-10-31]; the Fast DDS profile is an empty default participant [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/fastdds_rpi.xml#L5, acc. 2026-10-31]. [UNKNOWN: manual contains no statement about DDS security either way].
- **Remote access / cloud.** No cloud or telemetry endpoint in any TB4 repo; the only outbound network use in turtlebot4_setup is NTP pools and `apt` (grep for `http`/`curl` in turtlebot4_setup: firmware update POST to the Create 3 at 192.168.186.2, ntpd restart POST, Create 3 docs URLs) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/scripts/create_update.sh#L30, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/aliases.bash#L10, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L16-L17, acc. 2026-10-31]. Create 3 webserver reachable via Pi port 8080 (`socat` forward) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/systemd/system/webserver.service#L9, acc. 2026-10-31].
- **Personal data.** Camera images (`oakd/rgb/...`), lidar scans and SLAM maps are published on the network and maps are written to disk on whichever machine saves them [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_robot/blob/28e115f86930bb3273f1a1aa4738dbd8747513f0/turtlebot4_diagnostics/turtlebot4_diagnostics/diagnostics_updater.py#L82, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/tutorials/generate_map.md#L155, acc. 2026-10-31]; Wi-Fi credentials are stored in clear text in `/etc/netplan/50-wifis.yaml` by `conf.py` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/turtlebot4_setup/conf.py#L302, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/netplan/50-wifis.yaml#L10, acc. 2026-10-31]; the robot's IP is broadcast on `/ip` every 5 s [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4/blob/7fd29fb420e906f3aca4a904adb54b69b11c7c00/turtlebot4_node/src/turtlebot4.cpp#L298-L300, acc. 2026-10-31]. The Autonomy Meter log needs none of these (only command/state topics).

### 12. Security, privacy, update path (base part)  _[Create 3 base track]_

- Webserver authentication: the docs describe plain http on port 80 reachable on `usb0`, `wlan0` and the `wlan1` access point with no credential step anywhere [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/overview.md#L8; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/overview.md#L11, acc. 2026-10-31]; "Reboot robot" and "Factory reset" appear only in AP mode [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/application.md#L3; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/about.md#L40, acc. 2026-10-31]. Whether any authentication exists: [UNKNOWN: not described; absence of a login is not asserted by iRobot].
- Wi-Fi provisioning: AP mode by holding buttons 1 & 2 for 3 s; SSID `Create-[xxx]`; page at 192.168.10.1; 2.4 GHz only; region selection [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/provision.md#L8; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/provision.md#L13; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/connect.md#L2; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/connect.md#L10-L13, acc. 2026-10-31]. A hidden `/wpa-supp-override` page accepts a raw `wpa_supplicant.conf` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/wpa-supp-override.md#L10; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/wpa-supp-override.md#L13, acc. 2026-10-31].
- USB-Ethernet: fixed 192.168.186.2 on `usb0`; `/24` subnet changeable (beta) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/overview.md#L9; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/set-wired-subnet.md#L11, acc. 2026-10-31].
- Personal data on the base: Wi-Fi SSIDs and pre-shared keys (`wpa_supplicant.conf`), hostname and Bluetooth name, NTP config; "Factory Reset ... will remove all user configuration" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/wpa-supp-override.md#L21; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/connect.md#L8; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/about.md#L41, acc. 2026-10-31]; "Forget Wi-Fi" clears SSIDs [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/forget-wifi.md#L9, acc. 2026-10-31]. MAC addresses are shown on the About page [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/releases/g_2_2.md#L22, acc. 2026-10-31]. The base records no sensor logs beyond the volatile system log [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/logs.md#L6, acc. 2026-10-31].
- Firmware update path: webserver Update page — online download from iRobot when the robot has Internet, or upload of a local `.swu` file; the robot must stay on the dock [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/update.md#L8; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/update.md#L12; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/update.md#L16; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/hw/face.md#L63, acc. 2026-10-31]. Signing/verification of `.swu` images: [UNKNOWN: not documented].
- ROS 2 security (SROS2): no mention of SROS2, security enclaves or DDS-Security anywhere in create3_docs (grep for "SROS", "security", "secur" returned only a Wi-Fi sentence) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/network-config.md#L83, acc. 2026-10-31] → support is [UNKNOWN: undocumented; presume unsupported].
- Known risk: the robot restarts its ROS 2 application on connectivity changes (G.2.2 known issue; DHCP renewals with the same address exempted in G.3.1) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/releases/g_2_2.md#L51; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/releases/g_3_1.md#L41, acc. 2026-10-31]; current status in I.0.0 [UNKNOWN].

### 12. Security references (external part)  _[Evidence track]_

**ROS 2 / SROS2.** The Jazzy security tutorial states that "The sros2 package provides the tools and instructions to use ROS 2 on top of DDS-Security" and that security is turned on per terminal with `ROS_SECURITY_KEYSTORE`, `ROS_SECURITY_ENABLE=true` and `ROS_SECURITY_STRATEGY=Enforce`; keys and certificates are created per node enclave with `ros2 security create_enclave` [VERIFIED: https://docs.ros.org/en/jazzy/Tutorials/Advanced/Security/Introducing-ros2-security.html, acc. 2026-11-03]. The default is off: in `rcl` the flag is initialised `bool use_security = false;` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rcl/blob/18d96c9c2b98e616324ae9a2c7701dd0b0886ff0/rcl/src/rcl/security.c#L34, acc. 2026-11-03] and becomes true only when the environment variable equals the string `"true"` (`*use_security = (0 == strcmp(ros_security_enable, "true"));`) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rcl/blob/18d96c9c2b98e616324ae9a2c7701dd0b0886ff0/rcl/src/rcl/security.c#L82, acc. 2026-11-03; commit 18d96c9 = jazzy branch head on 2026-11-03]. The ROS 2 DDS-Security design article (https://design.ros2.org/articles/ros2_dds_security.html) could not be opened [UNKNOWN: host blocked]. Neither the TurtleBot 4 setup tool, the user manual nor the Create 3 docs mention `ROS_SECURITY_*`, `sros2` or enclaves (grep at the pinned commits) [VERIFIED: local grep, acc. 2026-10-31]; a passive observer should therefore assume plaintext, unauthenticated DDS on the robot's Wi-Fi unless the operator has enabled SROS2 [analyst inference].

**OMG DDS-Security 1.1.** Specification URL https://www.omg.org/spec/DDS-SECURITY/1.1/ [CLAIM: host blocked, not opened].

**Fast DDS discovery server.** Documentation URL https://fast-dds.docs.eprosima.com/en/latest/fastdds/discovery/discovery_server.html [CLAIM: host blocked, not opened]. The Create 3 docs' RMW-profile override page exists at the pinned commit (https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/webserver/rmw-profile-override/) [VERIFIED path exists, acc. 2026-10-31; content not relied upon here].

**Create 3 webserver.** "The Create® 3 robot runs a configuration webserver for modifying settings of the robot", reachable on `usb0` (192.168.186.2), `wlan0` (DHCP) or `wlan1` (192.168.10.1), and "The webserver is available over http (port 80)" [VERIFIED: https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/webserver/overview/ and https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/overview.md#L6-L11, acc. 2026-10-31]. The overview page describes no login, credential or TLS step, and a grep of create3_docs for `authenticat` and `password` finds only the Wi-Fi network password field [VERIFIED: local grep, acc. 2026-10-31]; whether the webserver is unauthenticated is therefore an inference from absence in the docs, to be confirmed by the base track [tagged UNKNOWN here]. The webserver exposes the application ROS 2 parameters file, from which start-up-only motion parameters are loaded: `publish_odom_tfs` "must be configured from the ROS 2 parameters file on the application configuration page of the webserver" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/ros2.md#L172, acc. 2026-10-31] and the `/wheels_stop_threshold_sec` velocity-timeout parameter likewise [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/moving-the-robot.md#L31-L33, acc. 2026-10-31]. Whether `safety_override` or `reflexes_enabled` can also be preset there is not stated on those lines [UNKNOWN]. Security relevance: anyone on the robot's network segment can edit motion-control start-up parameters through port 80 without a ROS 2 client [analyst inference].

**Personal data.** The OAK-D images and SLAM maps recorded by the stack can contain identifiable persons and home layouts. GDPR Art. 4(1): personal data is "any information relating to an identified or identifiable natural person", identifiable "directly or indirectly, in particular by reference to … location data" [VERIFIED: Regulation (EU) 2016/679 Art. 4(1), https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/679/oj read via http://publications.europa.eu/resource/cellar/3e485e15-11bd-11e6-ba9a-01aa75ed71a1.0006.03/DOC_1, acc. 2026-11-03]. gdpr-info.eu was not opened [host blocked]. An autonomy log that records only control states, timestamps and hashes is not personal data by itself; the bags it is derived from may be [analyst inference].

## 13. Regulation touching logging or human oversight

_Lead note._ The brief's working date for the Machinery Regulation (20 January 2027) is corrected by the Official Journal text to **14 January 2027** (Art. 54). Standards catalogues (ISO, ANSI/A3, UL, OMG) were not reachable; those entries are CLAIM by title only.

### 13. Regulation touching logging or human oversight  _[Evidence track]_

Scope framing: the TurtleBot 4 is an indoor research/education mobile robot with a Create 3 firmware speed cap of 0.306 m/s by default and 0.460 m/s with safety overridden [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/safety.md#L67, #L69, acc. 2026-10-31].

#### 13.1 EU Machinery Regulation (EU) 2023/1230 (read in full from the Official Journal XHTML)

- Applicability: "It shall apply from 14 January 2027" (Art. 54); Directive 2006/42/EC "is repealed with effect from 14 January 2027" and references to it are construed as references to the Regulation (Art. 51(2)) [VERIFIED: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2023/1230/oj via http://publications.europa.eu/resource/cellar/51ed7804-1617-11ee-806b-01aa75ed71a1.0006.03/DOC_1, acc. 2026-11-03]. **Conflict note:** the project brief gives 20 January 2027; the Official Journal text says 14 January 2027 — the text governs.
- Scope, Art. 2(1): applies to machinery and the listed related products and to partly completed machinery. Art. 2(2)(m) excludes "machinery or related products specially designed and constructed for research purposes for temporary use in laboratories" [VERIFIED: same]. A TurtleBot 4 sold as a product is machinery (it is placed on the market, not laboratory-built); a lab-modified TB4 used only temporarily in a lab may fall under the (m) exclusion — whether a commercially sold education robot qualifies is a legal question [analyst inference, UNKNOWN].
- Annex III Part B 1.1.6 Ergonomics, point (e): avoid "monitoring that requires lengthy concentration"; point (f): adapt the human-machine interface to operators of machinery "designed to operate with varying levels of autonomy"; point (g): such machinery should "communicate its planned actions … to operators in a comprehensible manner" [VERIFIED: Annex III 1.1.6(e)–(g), same source].
- Annex III 1.1.9 Protection against corruption: connection of a remote device must not lead to a hazardous situation; hardware transmitting safety-critical signals must be "adequately protected against accidental or intentional corruption"; the machinery "shall collect evidence of a legitimate or illegitimate intervention in that hardware component"; safety-critical software and data "shall be identified as such" and protected; the machinery shall identify the software necessary for safe operation [VERIFIED: Annex III 1.1.9, same source]. Relevance: an append-only hash-chained control log is one way to "collect evidence of … intervention"; the Autonomy Meter log records control changes, which is adjacent to but not the same as evidence of tampering with safety hardware [analyst inference].
- Annex III 1.2.1 Safety and reliability of control systems, first list point (f): "the tracing log of the data generated in relation to an intervention and of the versions of safety software uploaded" after placing on the market "is enabled for five years after such upload", for conformity demonstration on request of a national authority [VERIFIED: Annex III 1.2.1(f), same source]. Second list (control systems "with fully or partially self-evolving behaviour or logic that are designed to operate with varying levels of autonomy"): (a) no actions beyond defined task and movement space; (b) "recording of data on the safety related decision-making process for software based safety systems ensuring safety function … is enabled and that such data is retained for one year after its collection"; (c) it must be possible at all times to correct the machinery to maintain inherent safety [VERIFIED: Annex III 1.2.1 second list (a)–(c), same source]. Also 1.2.1(d) of the first list: no modifications to settings or rules generated by the machinery or operators "including during the machinery or related product learning phase, where such modifications could lead to hazardous situations" [VERIFIED].
- Annex I Part A items 5 and 6 (third-party conformity assessment): "Safety components with fully or partially self-evolving behaviour using machine learning approaches ensuring safety functions" and machinery with such embedded systems, "in respect only of those systems" [VERIFIED: Annex I Part A 5–6, same source]. Nav2's planners/controllers and the Create 3 reflexes are not described by their makers as machine-learning safety functions; the stock TB4 therefore does not obviously fall under Annex I 5–6 [analyst inference]. Recital 55: those provisions "should not apply to software incapable of learning or evolving" [VERIFIED].
- Mapping to the Autonomy Meter: the 1.2.1(f) "tracing log … in relation to an intervention" and the one-year retention of safety decision data in 1.2.1 second list (b) are the closest statutory hooks for an intervention log on autonomous machinery in the EU; the Regulation does not define "intervention" [VERIFIED absence: the word is used in 1.1.9 and 1.2.1(f) without a definition in Art. 3, same source].

#### 13.2 EU AI Act, Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (read in full from the Official Journal XHTML)

- Art. 2(6): the Regulation "does not apply to AI systems or AI models … specifically developed and put into service for the sole purpose of scientific research and development"; Art. 2(8): nor to "any research, testing or development activity regarding AI systems … prior to their being placed on the market or put into service", but "Testing in real world conditions shall not be covered by that exclusion"; Art. 2(12): nor to free and open-source AI systems unless placed on the market as high-risk or Art. 5/50 systems [VERIFIED: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj via http://publications.europa.eu/resource/cellar/dc8116a1-3fe6-11ef-865a-01aa75ed71a1.0006.03/DOC_1, acc. 2026-11-03].
- Art. 6(1): an AI system is high-risk if it is a safety component of (or is) a product covered by Annex I legislation **and** that product requires third-party conformity assessment under that legislation; Annex I Section A item 1 lists Directive 2006/42/EC on machinery [VERIFIED: Art. 6(1), Annex I A.1, same source]. Via Reg. 2023/1230 Art. 51(2), references to 2006/42/EC are to be read as references to the Machinery Regulation from 14 January 2027 [VERIFIED: Machinery Regulation Art. 51(2)]. Because a stock TB4 is not in Machinery Regulation Annex I (no ML safety function), Art. 6(1) is not obviously triggered [analyst inference]. Annex III (Art. 6(2)) lists areas such as biometrics and critical infrastructure; indoor research navigation is not listed [VERIFIED: Annex III headings, same source].
- Art. 12 Record-keeping: high-risk systems "shall technically allow for the automatic recording of events (logs) over the lifetime of the system"; logging must enable identification of risk situations, post-market monitoring and deployer monitoring under Art. 26(5) [VERIFIED: Art. 12(1)–(2), same source].
- Art. 14 Human oversight: oversight measures "shall be commensurate with the risks, level of autonomy and context of use"; persons assigned oversight must be able "to intervene in the operation of the high-risk AI system or interrupt the system through a 'stop' button or a similar procedure" and to "disregard, override or reverse the output" [VERIFIED: Art. 14(3), 14(4)(d)–(e), same source].
- Dates, Art. 113: applies from 2 August 2026; Art. 6(1) and corresponding obligations from 2 August 2027 [VERIFIED: Art. 113(c), same source].
- Art. 2(7): EU data-protection law applies to personal data processed in connection with the Regulation [VERIFIED].

#### 13.3 Standards (catalogue hosts blocked)

| Standard | Relevance | Tag |
|---|---|---|
| ISO 13482:2014 Robots and robotic devices — Safety requirements for personal care robots (mobile servant robot type) | closest voluntary safety standard for a small indoor mobile robot near people; paywalled | CLAIM: https://www.iso.org/standard/53820.html not opened (host blocked); title from analyst knowledge |
| ISO 3691-4:2023 Industrial trucks — driverless industrial trucks and their systems | not applicable: scoped to industrial trucks (load handling in industrial settings); TB4 is a research/education robot without load-handling function | CLAIM: https://www.iso.org/standard/83540.html not opened; non-applicability is analyst inference from the title scope |
| ANSI/RIA R15.08-1-2020 Industrial mobile robots — safety requirements, Part 1 | industrial mobile robots; TB4 is non-industrial | CLAIM: https://www.automate.org/ (A3) not opened |
| ISO 10218-1/-2 Industrial robots | not applicable: scoped to industrial manipulators and their integration | CLAIM / analyst inference |
| ISO 13849-1, IEC 62061 Functional safety of safety-related control systems | would apply to rated safety functions; the Create 3 docs call the bump/cliff behaviours "reflexes" and the backup limit / speed limit "safety features" but make no statement about a performance level, SIL or any functional-safety rating | Create 3 wording VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/reflexes.md#L1-L3, https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/safety.md#L3-L4, acc. 2026-10-31; rating UNKNOWN (no statement either way) |
| UL 3300 Outline of Investigation for Service, Communication, Information, Education and Entertainment Robots | the only North-American scheme naming education robots | CLAIM: https://www.shopulstandards.com/ not opened |

#### 13.4 United States

No federal regulation imposes logging or human-oversight requirements on an indoor research/education mobile robot of this class: it is not a motor vehicle (NHTSA), not a workplace machine subject to a specific OSHA standard unless used in a workplace, and not a consumer product with a specific CPSC rule [analyst inference; no primary text asserts this negative — tagged as the analyst's own reasoning, not evidence]. California DMV autonomous-vehicle testing regulations (13 CCR § 227.00 et seq.) apply to vehicles on public roads and not to an indoor robot [analyst inference; definitions.md already cites § 227.50 for the disengagement definition]. Radio: the Create 3 Wi-Fi/Bluetooth radio would be subject to FCC Part 15 equipment authorisation; the FCC ID is **not** stated anywhere in create3_docs at the pinned commit (grep for `FCC`, `regulat`, `certif` found only "unregulated battery port") [VERIFIED absence: local grep, acc. 2026-10-31; FCC ID UNKNOWN; 47 CFR Part 15 text at https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-47/chapter-I/subchapter-A/part-15 not opened, host blocked].

### 14B. Clocks: every timestamp field, its clock, resolution and sync

_Lead note._ Three clocks matter: the Create 3 system clock (ntpd, synced to the Pi at 192.168.186.3 or to Wi-Fi NTP; may refuse large jumps until reboot), the Pi system clock (chrony, `local stratum 10` fallback — free-running without internet, no RTC on a Pi 4 inferred, not stated), and the PC running Nav2/RViz. `BehaviorTreeStatusChange.timestamp` is BehaviorTree.CPP's own clock and does not follow `/clock` in simulation; `TransitionEvent.timestamp` is never assigned by rcl_lifecycle. The event log's `ts` is always the observer's UTC clock; source stamps go to `x_src_ts` with `x_src_clock` = `wall` or `sim`.

### 14B. Timestamps  _[Create 3 base track]_

- Clock: "it is publishing its data with the timestamp of its system clock, which synchronizes with an NTP server. If the Create 3's Wi-Fi is connected to a network with internet connection, it will sync to a global time NTP server. The Create 3 NTP config is also set to listen for servers on USB IP address 192.168.186.1 and 192.168.186.3" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L6; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L7; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L8; https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/setup/compute-ntp/, acc. 2026-10-31]. iRobot recommends a chrony server on the compute board, added to the robot's `ntp.conf` as `server 192.168.186.3 iburst` [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L15; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L39, acc. 2026-10-31]; "if there is a large jump in the time, the Create 3 may not accept it until its next reboot" (log line `ntpd: reply from 192.168.186.3: delay ### is too high, ignoring`) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L53; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L56, acc. 2026-10-31]. Known issue: "Robot prefers time sync from Wi-Fi over its USB interface" [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/releases/g_2_2.md#L52, acc. 2026-10-31]. Beta controls: set date/time (`POST /api/set-datetime/newdatetime=YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss`), edit `ntp.conf`, restart ntpd (`POST /api/restart-ntpd`) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/set-datetime.md#L13; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/edit-ntp-conf.md#L10; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/webserver/restart-ntpd.md#L11, acc. 2026-10-31]. Whether the TurtleBot 4 Pi image serves NTP to the base is the TB4 track's question.
- Fields: every custom message carries `std_msgs/Header header` (sec + nanosec, so nominal ns resolution; actual clock resolution [UNKNOWN]): `HazardDetectionVector.header` and each `HazardDetection.header` ("the timestamp will denote when the robot detected the hazard and the frame id will denote the location of the sensor") [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/hazards.md#L18; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/hazards.md#L19; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/HazardDetection.msg#L20, acc. 2026-10-31]; `IrOpcode.header` ("time when we saw the opcode") [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/IrOpcode.msg#L15, acc. 2026-10-31]; `Mouse.header` ("acquisition time of mouse measure") [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/Mouse.msg#L3, acc. 2026-10-31]; `WheelStatus/WheelTicks/WheelVels.header` ("acquisition time of measure") [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/WheelStatus.msg#L3; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/WheelTicks.msg#L4; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/WheelVels.msg#L3, acc. 2026-10-31]; `DockStatus.header` ("when dock info was queried") [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/DockStatus.msg#L3, acc. 2026-10-31]; `InterfaceButtons.header` ("time at which information was collected") and per-button `last_start_pressed_time` (Time) and `last_pressed_duration` (Duration) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/InterfaceButtons.msg#L2; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/Button.msg#L5; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/Button.msg#L6, acc. 2026-10-31]; `KidnapStatus`, `StopStatus`, `SlipStatus`, `IrIntensityVector` headers (no semantics stated) [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/KidnapStatus.msg#L3; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/StopStatus.msg#L3; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/SlipStatus.msg#L3, acc. 2026-10-31]. `KidnapStatus` stamp was wrong before G.5.3/H.2.3 [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/releases/g_5_3.md#L24, acc. 2026-10-31]. In the hazards example, the vector header and detection headers differ by ~0.1 ms and detection stamps are shared per sensor group [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/hazards.md#L39; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/hazards.md#L46; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/hazards.md#L70, acc. 2026-10-31].
- For the event log: `ts` = observer UTC time; keep the firmware stamp as `x_src_ts` with `x_src_clock: wall`, and log the chrony/ntp offset between the Pi and the base at session start (the base's own offset is only visible in its log page).

| `header.stamp` from Nav2 collision_monitor / velocity_smoother | node clock on the machine running Nav2 (PC recommended) | ns | PC's NTP | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4-user-manual/blob/4455a75dde3f052b5abf615dade22bece84120b0/software/overview.md#L26, acc. 2026-10-31] (Nav2 track for the stamping code) |
| `Joy.header.stamp` | joy_linux node clock, frame_id "joy" | ns | Pi chrony | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-drivers/joystick_drivers/blob/038a12722bac3983d7fee462ad074a8f18c7bd5f/joy_linux/src/joy_linux_node.cpp#L379, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| Create 3 message stamps | Create 3 system clock synced by NTP to the Pi (192.168.186.3) or Wi-Fi | ns | Create 3 ntpd | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L6, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/compute-ntp.md#L8, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://iroboteducation.github.io/create3_docs/setup/compute-ntp/, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| Pi system clock | chrony: Ubuntu pools, `local stratum 10`, `rtcsync`, `makestep 1 3`, serves 192.168.186.0/24 | — | internet NTP when available | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L16-L17, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L26, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L47, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/etc/turtlebot4/chrony.conf#L51, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_setup/blob/5fe58657aeff57d28cb2cfb4f0c6cc6c62a4b96c/debian/postinst#L6, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| rosbag2 `recv_timestamp` (MCAP `logTime`) | recorder host clock at reception (`received_timestamp` from rmw) | ns | observer's NTP | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/rosbag2_transport/src/rosbag2_transport/recorder.cpp#L801, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/rosbag2_storage_mcap/src/mcap_storage.cpp#L873, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| rosbag2 `send_timestamp` (MCAP `publishTime`) | publisher host clock (`source_timestamp` from rmw) | ns | publisher's NTP | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/rosbag2_transport/src/rosbag2_transport/recorder.cpp#L802, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/rosbag2_storage_mcap/src/mcap_storage.cpp#L874, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| rosbag2 with `--use-sim-time` | `recv` = `node->now()` (sim clock) | ns | /clock | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros2/rosbag2/blob/cb8fa198b93bfed90e2417b52b8645fb9ef89c2b/rosbag2_transport/src/rosbag2_transport/recorder.cpp#L786, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| Gazebo sim | `/clock` bridged from gz; all TB4 sim launches `use_sim_time:=true` | ns | — | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_simulator/blob/b7d0f3b973258481f6cd8137bef9240fb567f566/turtlebot4_gz_bringup/launch/sim.launch.py#L109, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_simulator/blob/b7d0f3b973258481f6cd8137bef9240fb567f566/turtlebot4_gz_bringup/launch/turtlebot4_spawn.launch.py#L36, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| Button long-press timing in turtlebot4_node | `std::chrono::steady_clock` (monotonic) | ms thresholds (2000/3000 ms) | n/a | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4/blob/7fd29fb420e906f3aca4a904adb54b69b11c7c00/turtlebot4_node/include/turtlebot4_node/buttons.hpp#L119, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4/blob/7fd29fb420e906f3aca4a904adb54b69b11c7c00/turtlebot4_node/include/turtlebot4_node/buttons.hpp#L131, acc. 2026-10-31] |

Autonomy Meter rule: `ts` = observer wall clock (UTC, NTP-disciplined); keep `header.stamp` as `x_src_ts` with `x_src_clock` = `wall` (hardware) or `sim` (Gazebo); the chrony `local stratum 10` fallback means a Pi without internet keeps a free-running clock, so `received_timestamp - source_timestamp` must be logged per publisher as a skew diagnostic.

| Field | Clock | Resolution | Notes | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BehaviorTreeLog.timestamp` | node clock of the BT client node = ROS time (`use_sim_time` → simulation clock in Gazebo, wall clock on the robot) | ns (`builtin_interfaces/Time`) | Set to `clock_->now()` at flush time, i.e. up to one BT loop (10 ms) after the events it carries | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_behavior_tree/include/nav2_behavior_tree/ros_topic_logger.hpp#L91-L99, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_behavior_tree/include/nav2_behavior_tree/bt_action_server_impl.hpp#L125-L136, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| `BehaviorTreeStatusChange.timestamp` | BehaviorTree.CPP's internal event timestamp converted with `tf2_ros::toMsg(tf2::TimePoint(timestamp))`; the .msg comment says "Typically this is wall clock time" | ns | **Not** ROS time: in simulation it does not follow `/clock`. BehaviorTree.CPP's `TreeNode::setStatus` notifies with `std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now()` | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_behavior_tree/include/nav2_behavior_tree/ros_topic_logger.hpp#L69-L71, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_msgs/msg/BehaviorTreeStatusChange.msg#L1, acc. 2026-10-31] [CLAIM: https://github.com/BehaviorTree/BehaviorTree.CPP/blob/a9d6e5637f4a4fcd29fcd16b8db049de7c42006b/src/tree_node.cpp#L189 - BT.CPP master branch, not the Jazzy-pinned release, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| `NavigateToPose` feedback `navigation_time` | `clock_->now() - start_time_` on the bt_navigator node clock (ROS time) | ns duration | `start_time_` reset on every accepted/preempted goal | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_bt_navigator/src/navigators/navigate_to_pose.cpp#L191, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_bt_navigator/src/navigators/navigate_to_pose.cpp#L258, acc. 2026-10-31] |

| `TwistStamped.header.stamp` on `/cmd_vel_smoothed` | copied from the input command's stamp (default `stamp_smoothed_velocity_with_smoothing_time: false`); if the input stamp is zero, `last_command_time_ = now()` | ns | After a timeout the synthetic zero command is stamped `now()` | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_velocity_smoother/src/velocity_smoother.cpp#L320-L329, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_velocity_smoother/src/velocity_smoother.cpp#L241-L246, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_velocity_smoother/src/velocity_smoother.cpp#L337-L338, acc. 2026-10-31] |

| `use_sim_time` | not in `nav2.yaml`; launch default `false` on the robot, `true` in the Gazebo bring-up | — | Governs every "node clock" row above | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4/blob/7fd29fb420e906f3aca4a904adb54b69b11c7c00/turtlebot4_navigation/launch/nav2.launch.py#L34-L36, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4_simulator/blob/b7d0f3b973258481f6cd8137bef9240fb567f566/turtlebot4_gz_bringup/launch/turtlebot4_spawn.launch.py#L36-L38, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_bringup/launch/navigation_launch.py#L128, acc. 2026-10-31] |
| Collision monitor `source_timeout` | node clock (`this->now()`) compared with sensor stamps | 1.0 s on TB4 | A scan older than 1.0 s triggers the "invalid source" STOP | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_collision_monitor/src/collision_monitor_node.cpp#L416, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/ros-navigation/navigation2/blob/d5d768b7b38a05e7cfd2b6c693b786b402161bed/nav2_collision_monitor/src/collision_monitor_node.cpp#L437-L448, acc. 2026-10-31] [VERIFIED: https://github.com/turtlebot/turtlebot4/blob/7fd29fb420e906f3aca4a904adb54b69b11c7c00/turtlebot4_navigation/config/nav2.yaml#L303, acc. 2026-10-31] |

Consequence for the event log: `x_src_ts` may be copied from `BehaviorTreeLog.timestamp` with `x_src_clock = sim` or `wall` depending on `use_sim_time`; `BehaviorTreeStatusChange.timestamp` must not be used as `x_src_ts` in simulation.

| Firmware button holds (standby 10 s, storage 7 s, AP mode 3 s) | preceding `/interface_buttons` pattern, then loss of all base topics; cyan/blue light ring for AP mode | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/hw/face.md#L10; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/hw/face.md#L22; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/setup/provision.md#L8; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/hw/face.md#L46, acc. 2026-10-31] |

| Another cmd_vel publisher (e.g. a laptop on the same domain) | `ros2 topic info -v` publisher count > 1 on the robot-side topic; `wheel_vels` disagreeing with the observed Nav2 command | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/create3_docs/blob/9fb1510bbceb0e01c4764ddc458da59487519dca/docs/api/moving-the-robot.md#L26, acc. 2026-10-31] |

| Physical push / carry | `wheel_vels`/`odom` motion with zero PWM in `wheel_status`; `kidnap_status` | [VERIFIED: https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/WheelStatus.msg#L6; https://github.com/iRobotEducation/irobot_create_msgs/blob/b4986ef5dc2e07d8aa7471f29c56b617473352c5/msg/KidnapStatus.msg#L4, acc. 2026-10-31] |
